People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. So, that's why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing voices when no one else is around?\n\nPatient: No. ...I don't hear things like that. Just... no.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling unusually high or energized lately?\n\nPatient: No. I don't... I haven't been feeling like that. Just... normal, I guess. Not high or energized.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and sending notes about you?\n\nPatient: ...About a year. Maybe a year and a month. Something like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least a couple of weeks when you felt very depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I guess so. Most mornings... I wake up and there's just this... heavy feeling. Like it's hollow. It's like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. So... yeah, mostly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt watched or targeted even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. There was a... a spell of a few weeks, maybe. I was eating a bit better. Wasn't waking up quite so... hopeless, I guess. But... they were still watching my door. The neighbors. Reporting on me. That didn't change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you went away completely?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not really. It's... it's always there. Even if I don't see them looking, I know they are. It never really stops.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess I don't talk much anyway. There's just... not much to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a seizure?\n\nPatient: ...No. I haven't. Never had one of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you care much less about things than you used to?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I suppose. I used to do things. Choir practice... playing bridge. I'd cook beans on Sundays, do little repair jobs for folks around here. But... I don't know. Now I mostly just let the phone ring. Just... tell myself there's no point going.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: ...I guess sometimes. People give me this look. Like... like I skipped half the story. I'll try to explain something, you know... I'll start talking about the rent, and then... the marina, and the choir robes... and the wires in the walls. And they just stare at me. So I just... don't say much anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your face seems less expressive or that your voice sounds flat?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so. Nobody's said anything like that to me. But... like I said, I don't really see people enough for them to notice anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you had problems with your memory?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not really. I remember things okay, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are wires in the walls that are being used to monitor or control you?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know what you mean. I just... I know there are wires. That's just how buildings are made. I never said anything about... being monitored.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone can control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: ...No. Nobody controls what I think. That doesn't... that doesn't really make sense. They're just my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when it felt hard to start or keep doing everyday activities, even simple things?\n\nPatient: ...No. I do what needs doing. It's... just regular stuff. I get by alright, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Not high or energized.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling that people at your apartment are watching you and sending notes about you?\n\nPatient: ...About a year. Maybe a year and a month. Something like that.\n\nDoctor: During this past year, have you had a period of at least a couple of weeks when you felt very depressed most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I guess so. Most mornings... I wake up and there's just this... heavy feeling. Like it's hollow. It's like the day already went bad before I even get my feet on the floor. So... yeah, mostly every day.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt watched or targeted even when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. There was a... a spell of a few weeks, maybe. I was eating a bit better. Wasn't waking up quite so... hopeless, I guess. But... they were still watching my door. The neighbors. Reporting on me. That didn't change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when the feeling that people were watching or reporting on you went away completely?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not really. It's... it's always there. Even if I don't see them looking, I know they are. It never really stops.\n\nDoctor: Over the past year, have you found yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know. I guess I don't talk much anyway. There's just... not much to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a seizure?\n\nPatient: ...No. I haven't. Never had one of those.\n\nDoctor: Have you found that you care much less about things than you used to?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah. I suppose. I used to do things. Choir practice... playing bridge. I'd cook beans on Sundays, do little repair jobs for folks around here. But... I don't know. Now I mostly just let the phone ring. Just... tell myself there's no point going.\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech is hard to follow or that you jump from one idea to another?\n\nPatient: ...I guess sometimes. People give me this look. Like... like I skipped half the story. I'll try to explain something, you know... I'll start talking about the rent, and then... the marina, and the choir robes... and the wires in the walls. And they just stare at me. So I just... don't say much anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have people told you that your face seems less expressive or that your voice sounds flat?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't think so. Nobody's said anything like that to me. But... like I said, I don't really see people enough for them to notice anyway.\n\nDoctor: Have you had problems with your memory?\n\nPatient: ...No. Not really. I remember things okay, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe there are wires in the walls that are being used to monitor or control you?\n\nPatient: ...I don't know what you mean. I just... I know there are wires. That's just how buildings are made. I never said anything about... being monitored.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe anyone can control your thoughts?\n\nPatient: ...No. Nobody controls what I think. That doesn't... that doesn't really make sense. They're just my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when it felt hard to start or keep doing everyday activities, even simple things?\n\nPatient: ...No. I do what needs doing. It's... just regular stuff. I get by alright, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Before all this started, were you generally able to manage your work, home responsibilities, and relationships normally?\n\nPatient: ...I guess things used to be easier. Lately... I don't know. I've missed some appointments. The bills pile up sometimes. I just... I haven't been going to church or doing my volunteer stuff. It's just hard to keep track of the days, and... I just don't really want to deal with people right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the people at your apartment can do things to you that would be impossible in ordinary life?\n\nPatient: ...No. I don't know what you mean by that. They're just... neighbors. They don't do anything to me. I just stay in my apartment.\n\nDoctor: During the past year, has that depressed mood been present for most of the time?\n\nPatient: ...Yeah, I guess. Since I moved, mostly. Even when my head isn't as... mixed up, I still just feel down. Like I'm no use to anybody. Just don't care about much anymore.\n\nDoctor: During that depressed time, did you lose interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: ...I suppose. I used to like doing things. Choir practice... playing bridge. I'd cook up a pot of beans on Sundays, maybe help folks out with little repair jobs around their places. But... lately, I just... I hear the phone ring and I just let it go. Tell myself there ain't no point in going anywhere anyway.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
